# 351: Chapter 352: Next

Mega Absol stood tall, the "wings" on its back gently rippling—majestic and solemn.

"Looks pretty tough. Alright then, let's see what you've got."

Selene was raring to go. This was her first time facing a Mega Evolved opponent.

After all, not many trainers in Kanto used Mega Evolution—it wasn't just rare; the stones were hard to come by.

"Earthquake!"

Because Venusaur was still under the effects of Taunt, it could only use offensive moves. No matter—it just had to wait it out.

Venusaur raised its front legs and then slammed them down hard.

Rumble!

The ground trembled violently, seismic waves blasting forward toward Mega Absol.

"Dodge it—Flamethrower!"

Instead of backing away, Mega Absol dashed forward, completely unfazed by the quaking terrain.

Its snowy fur and wing-like tufts fluttered magnificently in the wind as it ran.

Just as the shockwave was about to reach it, Mega Absol leapt into the air, flames already gathering in its mouth.

A thick stream of red-hot fire burst forth—Fire-type: Flamethrower!

Boom!

Flamethrower struck dead-on—Venusaur couldn't dodge while rooted by Ingrain. Flames exploded across its body, and Venusaur roared in pain—Fire counters Grass.

The roots pulsed beneath it, and green light shimmered around Venusaur's body—Ingrain was healing it.

Its injuries eased slightly.

"Psycho Cut!"

Mega Absol landed and immediately lunged forward again, lightning-fast.

The crescent horn on its head gleamed with razor-sharp psychic energy.

"Sludge Bomb!" Selene called out without hesitation.

Venusaur opened its mouth and launched blobs of toxic sludge—Poison-type: Sludge Bomb!

But Mega Absol showed no fear. With a swift sidestep, it slid gracefully to the side, completely evading the attack, then charged again.

Its movements were clean, precise, and effortless.

"It's just as agile as that Weavile."

Watching this, Selene frowned slightly in admiration.

Wham!

Mega Absol lunged and brought its shimmering psychic blade down hard—Psycho Cut slammed into Venusaur's forehead.

Venusaur roared in pain. Psychic-type moves counter Poison—and this one hit hard.

But again, the roots swelled, and green sparks rose from its body, healing it.

"Seed Bomb!"

Venusaur gritted through the pain and roared, spitting massive green seeds at the retreating Mega Absol.

"Dodge."

Mega Absol reared back, the seeds barely grazing its body.

Selene's eyes twitched. Wait—Mega Absol had no foothold midair!

Still, it twisted mid-fall, landed on its feet, skidded to a stop, and calmly looked back at Venusaur with its cold crimson eyes.

"Strong physical attacks… its physical strength must be way higher than its special."

Selene quickly assessed. That Psycho Cut had hit much harder than Flamethrower.

She still didn't know what Mega Absol's ability was, though…

Meanwhile, Ingrain kept steadily healing Venusaur. Though it couldn't move or retreat, the sustained healing was a solid advantage.

"Venusaur, Energy Ball!"

Despite the damage taken, Venusaur had great bulk and healing. It could hang in there.

Venusaur formed a glowing green orb and launched it at Mega Absol.

"Sucker Punch!"

Mega Absol's eyes flashed black, its body blurring forward—Dark-type priority move: Sucker Punch!

Smash!

It rammed into Venusaur, pushing it back. Venusaur growled at its opponent.

"Psycho Cut!"

"Leech Seed!"

Mega Absol's expression stayed icy, its horn glowing as it slashed down once more.

Venusaur, its flower trembling, scattered a spray of green seeds—Grass-type: Leech Seed.

That's right—Taunt had worn off. Venusaur could now use status moves again.

But just as the seeds were about to land—

A shimmering barrier appeared around Mega Absol.

Not only did it block Leech Seed—it reflected it.

"Magic Bounce!!"

Selene's eyes widened in realization.

Magic Bounce!

Reflects status moves targeting the user. Completely nullifies and rebounds certain support moves.

A powerful ability.

Of course Selene knew about it—Pokémon like Xatu and Espeon had it. So did Mega Sableye.

But she never expected Mega Absol to have it too.

The Leech Seed bounced back—but Venusaur, being Grass-type, was immune.

Lucky it wasn't Sleep Powder—that would've been trouble. Then again, Sleep Powder wouldn't affect Venusaur either.

Still… this was getting tough.

Mega Absol's next Psycho Cut struck clean—Venusaur roared again in pain.

Though Ingrain kept healing it, the damage was adding up fast.

A faint green glow now shimmered across Venusaur's body—Overgrow had activated. Its HP was critically low.

"Venusaur, Frenzy Plant!" Selene's brow furrowed. This wasn't looking good.

If that's the case… go all in.

Venusaur roared, the green glow intensifying.

Rumble!

The ground split open—giant, root-like vines exploded upward, thrashing wildly.

"Detect."

Dylan chuckled softly.

Mega Absol remained calm, trying to escape the attack radius—but the Frenzy Plant was massive.

Thick vines surged up on all sides, enclosing Mega Absol.

Under Venusaur's control, they shot toward their target with terrifying force.

Mega Absol leapt and twisted, dodging again and again. When it couldn't, its eyes glinted—

Perfect evade—Detect!

"Whoa!"

"This Absol is insane! I've never seen anyone dance through Frenzy Plant before!"

"So fast and elegant—but Selene's no joke either! I've watched her grow up—never thought she'd get this strong!"

The crowd was electric with excitement.

"Venusaur!"

Selene barked out a command.

Mega Absol kept weaving through the vines—but the space was shrinking fast.

Venusaur roared again. More roots burst up, tightening the net around its opponent.

BAM!

After so much dodging, Mega Absol finally took a direct hit to the back. It grunted, staggering.

The next moment, multiple vines smashed into it in a flurry of blows.

Mega Absol hit the ground hard.

Frenzy Plant ended, the roots withdrawing into the earth, leaving the field a mess of torn-up soil.

Venusaur stood panting, unable to move—Frenzy Plant's recoil: immobility.

But Mega Absol was still moving. It pushed itself up and charged forward again.

"Megahorn!"

Its yellow-green horn glowed brightly and slammed into the motionless Venusaur—Bug-type: Megahorn!

Venusaur's hefty frame was thrown clean off its roots—Ingrain cut off. The attack had serious weight behind it.

It hit the ground, groaning… then slowly went still.

"Venusaur is unable to battle. Mega Absol wins."

Leaf announced the result.

"Thanks for everything, Venusaur. Take a good rest."

Selene recalled it, glancing toward the elegant, freshly-shaken fur of Mega Absol—flawless once again.

Tch. A bit frustrating.

She clicked her tongue. If Frenzy Plant had landed earlier, the damage might've been enough.

But it only hit near the end. A few blows before the move ended—that wasn't ideal.

Still, it was something. She'd gotten a better grasp of Mega Absol's strengths.

"That Venusaur was impressive—but it's still no match for my Mega Absol."

Dylan offered a compliment. Selene had trained her Venusaur well. With Taunt and Magic Bounce shutting down many of its usual tricks, it still held up admirably.

Knowing Selene, she probably started with Ingrain just to be cheeky.

"Tch~ Then I'll just have to beat your Mega Absol next!"

Selene pouted, then threw out Incineroar again. Mega Absol's physical attacks were too strong—Intimidate would help.

Incineroar landed and roared, a wave of pressure spreading—Intimidate activated!

Mega Absol's Attack dropped by one stage.

"Return, Incineroar."

Selene recalled him right after—Magic Bounce could reflect a lot, but not trash talk. No reason to push her luck.

"Gallade! Get ready to fight!"

She tossed her fourth Poké Ball.

In a flash of light, Iron Valiant landed—standing tall with blade-arms glowing, eyes locked on Mega Absol.

A futuristic paradox Pokémon: Iron Valiant.

"Whoa~ it's the Iron Valiant Boss gave Selene back then!"

Asha blinked in surprise. She had high hopes for this cold, intimidating Pokémon—it was a gift from Dylan when Selene first set out.

"Gallade's really powerful. Good pick. With Magic Bounce, support moves are off the table. You have to fight it head-on."

Leaf nodded.

Status moves wouldn't work on Mega Absol and would just bounce back. A direct clash was the best bet.

Selene must've realized that too—hence Iron Valiant.

Even if she was usually more about trickery—uh, status tactics.

After all, the very first thing Dylan taught her was: use support moves smartly.

(End of Chapter)

---

---

---

# 352: Chapter 353: She’s Gym Leader Dylan’s Disciple

"Iron Valiant, huh? Looks like you've raised it well."

Dylan eyed the Iron Valiant from head to toe. Its aura was cold and sharp—proof of how well Selene had trained it.

But that wasn't surprising. Selene had always loved Iron Valiant's cool design—they just clicked.

And with a naturally high base stat total, Iron Valiant was definitely a top-tier battler. With proper training, it could even be a team's ace.

"A Fairy/Fighting-type Iron Valiant going up against a pure Dark-type Mega Absol. Just by typing, Iron Valiant has the upper hand."

In the stands, the older sister scanned Iron Valiant's data. Both of its types counter Dark, and both resist it.

That meant Iron Valiant had quadruple resistance to Dark-type moves.

On paper, Iron Valiant had a massive advantage against Mega Absol.

"Yeah, but battles aren't just about typing. Especially not against Gym Leader Dylan's Mega Absol."

The Psychic-type specialist stared intently at Iron Valiant. It had traits of both Gardevoir and Gallade—it looked amazing.

Too bad it didn't have Psychic typing.

On the field—

"Iron Valiant, Aura Sphere!"

Selene launched the first strike. With a type advantage, there was no reason to hesitate.

Iron Valiant spun its gleaming blade, forming a blue energy orb at the tip before firing it at Mega Absol.

Fighting-type: Aura Sphere!

"Psycho Cut!"

Mega Absol's horn glowed with psychic energy, forming a sharp blade that slashed through the air, cleaving the Aura Sphere in half—it exploded on impact.

With the attack broken, Mega Absol dashed forward on all fours toward Iron Valiant.

"Dazzling Gleam!"

Pink light sparked at the tip of Iron Valiant's blade and shot toward Mega Absol—Fairy-type: Dazzling Gleam!

"Sucker Punch!"

Mega Absol's eyes flashed with dark energy—its body became a blur of white as it sprinted forward with Dark-type priority move: Sucker Punch.

In a blink, it dodged the beam and was already up close.

"Brick Break!" Selene called instantly.

Iron Valiant sensed the incoming blow and slashed behind itself with one arm—Fighting-type: Brick Break!

But Mega Absol was fast. It saw the move coming and ducked.

It didn't need to land Sucker Punch—Dark moves barely scratched Iron Valiant. With that in mind, it focused entirely on dodging.

The deadly blade whistled just over Mega Absol's head.

A strong hit—but it missed. And worse—it left an opening.

"Iron Valiant!" Selene's expression tensed. She hadn't expected Mega Absol to give up the attack.

"Zen Headbutt!" Dylan grinned.

Mega Absol's horn shone with rainbow light—it lunged, headfirst.

Psychic-type: Zen Headbutt.

Iron Valiant stepped back but was too late—it crossed its blades in front to block.

Boom!

Mega Absol's forehead slammed into the crossed hilts, force vibrating down to Iron Valiant's chest.

It grunted, sliding backward. That hit wasn't light—Psychic moves were super effective.

Thankfully, Iron Valiant had decent physical defense.

"Absol, keep going—Air Slash!"

Dylan didn't waste the chance to follow up.

While Iron Valiant was a high-speed burst attacker, Mega Absol… was too.

Absol had always been popular—probably due to its cool, mysterious design and tragic lore.

In Dylan's past life, Absol was well-loved. But competitively, it was kind of a joke.

Aside from its Attack stat, the rest of its base stats were pretty bad. Especially Speed—it was in that awkward mid-zone that didn't work for trick room or fast teams.

Mega Evolution fixed some of that. Speed increased, and both Attack and Special Attack improved enough to build it into a mixed glass cannon.

That's the direction Dylan had trained his Absol in. Even without Mega, this Absol had exceptional agility and reaction time.

But Mega Absol? A different beast entirely.

Its crescent horn glowed white—it whipped it forward, slicing the air.

Flying-type: Air Slash!

"Protect!"

Selene reacted fast. Iron Valiant's green shield shimmered into place, absorbing the blow.

But the moment the shield faded, Mega Absol was already closing in again.

Iron Valiant crossed its arms, blades splitting into two as it extended them from both sides.

Its icy gaze locked on its opponent.

"Play Rough!"

"Close Combat!"

Mega Absol's claws and fangs lit with pink energy. Iron Valiant's twin blades glowed white.

They stepped in.

Mega Absol slashed down—Iron Valiant met the strike with its right blade.

Clash!

Mega Absol's Play Rough had the edge, even with its Attack lowered by Intimidate.

But just barely.

Right arm clashing, Iron Valiant's left blade slashed at Mega Absol's side—

Mega Absol bared its fangs and bit down, stopping the strike mid-swing.

Then—

Iron Valiant raised a leg and kicked it hard in the stomach.

Bam!

Mega Absol tumbled back, skidding to a stop and glaring at Iron Valiant with cold eyes.

That hurt.

"You raised it well," Dylan murmured, satisfied. Selene had clearly poured effort into this one.

Mega Absol still breathing hard, Iron Valiant stepped forward.

Its left arm swept wide—blades gleaming pink like a long katana.

Then—it ran.

Faster. And faster.

"That Iron Valiant is so freaking cool!!"

The Bird Keeper was wide-eyed. That calm, ruthless samurai-like stride—it was captivating.

"How is it this stylish?!"

"Dude, totally agree! It's insane!"

The Psychic-type trainer was starry-eyed.

"And it's strong! It's holding its own in melee against Mega Absol!"

The older sister clicked her tongue. That was no small feat.

Blade met claw, punch met fang—both Pokémon went all out in their close-quarters duel.

This Iron Valiant was seriously strong—against Dylan's Mega Absol, no less!

In just moments, both Pokémon were bruised and breathing heavily.

"Air Slash!"

"Aura Sphere!"

After one more Psycho Cut–Play Rough trade, both trainers issued new orders.

Mega Absol whipped its horn again—Air Slash tore through the air.

Iron Valiant raised an arm and launched a glowing Aura Sphere.

Boom!

The two moves collided, each slamming into their targets.

As the smoke cleared—

Iron Valiant lay motionless.

It had already taken two Close Combats, lowering its defenses. It couldn't withstand another super-effective Air Slash.

Mega Absol, meanwhile, was barely holding on. It collapsed to the ground, chest heaving.

A flash of light—its Mega form dissolved. It had fainted, too.

"Iron Valiant and Mega Absol are both unable to battle."

Leaf stepped in to confirm.

The crowd erupted again.

"Woah! Another double knockout! But that was so epic!"

"Selene's gotten so strong after her journey!"

"She's always been strong—don't forget who trained her!"

"Feels like Boss went easy on her though. That Absol shouldn't have gone down trading like that, even if Iron Valiant was intense…"

"That's normal. You'd know if you watched more Gym battles. Boss always holds back a bit."

This was the second double KO of the match.

But for many in the crowd, it was also the most thrilling round—their exchange of blows, that head-to-head clash of strength and agility… pure adrenaline.

On the field—

"Whew… You did amazing."

Selene returned Iron Valiant, exhaling deeply. She smiled at her Pokémon, proud and grateful.

Even though it fainted, it had brought down Dylan's Mega Absol—that alone was a massive achievement.

Mega Absol was terrifying—massive offensive power, immune to status moves. Not easy to deal with.

"Very strong, Selene. You raised Iron Valiant excellently."

Dylan gave his praise freely. Sure, he had let Mega Absol go toe-to-toe with Iron Valiant...

But the fact that Iron Valiant held its ground that well was all Selene's doing.

"Hehe~ Well, of course! I love Iron Valiant!"

Selene's sweet smile returned, full of pride—and not a shred of modesty.

"Alright then… next up, this little one again."

Dylan smiled, tossing another Poké Ball.

A flash of white light—Umbreon reappeared before him. It stared at Selene, crimson eyes calm and unshaken.

"That Umbreon again..."

Selene frowned slightly. She'd considered sending out Incineroar again, but there was no point using Intimidate on Umbreon.

This thing was just one big tank.

(End of Chapter)

---

# 353: Chapter 354: She’s Defeated Three of Dylan’s Pokémon

Selene took a moment to think. How should she deal with that Umbreon?

After a brief pause, she made up her mind.

"Weezing, get ready for battle!"

She tossed her fifth Poké Ball. In a flash of white light, a Weezing floated into place before her.

"Weezing, huh?"

Leaf rubbed her pale chin as she studied the floating dual-headed Pokémon.

Weezing wasn't exactly rare—especially considering how often Team Rocket used them in the past.

Beep~ Weezing, Poison-type, Poison Gas Pokémon.

Ability: Neutralizing Gas

Moves: Destiny Bond, Explosion, Toxic, Sludge Bomb, Haze, Heat Wave, Smog, Pain Split...

Description: Though it draws in less air than the Galarian form, the toxins it produces are even more potent.

Tch.

Leaf clicked her tongue. This Weezing was nothing like those used by Team Rocket grunts.

The move list? Straight from the abyss.

"She's going with Weezing to block Toxic, huh."

Dylan glanced at the Pokémon and smiled quietly.

Umbreon, with its low offense, was best used for stall and control strategies.

His Umbreon was no exception—an opener that could spread status with Toxic or Thunder Wave, or pivot with Baton Pass.

Dylan liked using it—it was reliable.

But today, Umbreon hadn't had many chances to shine. Selene had clearly come prepared for a stall-based strategy.

Dark misty gas began to spread from Weezing, quickly blanketing the battlefield.

Neutralizing Gas.

This ability suppressed all other abilities on the field.

A brutally annoying ability—many Pokémon relied on their abilities for power spikes, and this one neutralized that edge completely.

"Umbreon, Taunt," Dylan commanded decisively.

To be honest, when used right, Weezing could be a serious headache.

Especially with moves like Destiny Bond and Pain Split—classic Selene.

Umbreon let out a mocking cry, eyes locked on Weezing—Taunt activated.

"Weezing, Toxic Spikes!"

Selene moved fast.

A sinister purple sphere materialized above Weezing, then shot into the sky—

Right as Weezing's dopey eyes locked onto Umbreon in anger.

Taunt successful. But the Toxic Spikes were also deployed.

The orb exploded, showering the battlefield in droplets of toxic liquid.

The floor glowed purple for a moment—then vanished.

"Whoa~ Toxic Spikes, huh? Never thought I'd see Boss caught in one of his own tactics," the older sister grinned.

Toxic Spikes were Dylan's signature. The strategy that put him on the map—especially his battle against Red.

"Selene's smart. Trying to beat Umbreon head-on is tough—it's so tanky, especially with Thunder Wave and Toxic in its kit."

"But with Toxic Spikes set up, even Umbreon will get worn down eventually."

The Bird Keeper nodded in admiration—assuming Dylan didn't switch out, that is.

Though realistically, no way would Dylan just let the field stay poisoned.

"Toxic Spikes, huh?" Dylan chuckled softly.

"Baton Pass."

Umbreon's body glowed white and returned to its Poké Ball.

"Honchkrow, take the field!"

Dylan tossed his fourth Poké Ball.

An elegant, dark-winged figure flapped into the air—Honchkrow, Dark/Flying-type.

"Defog!"

"Thunderbolt!"

Dylan immediately gave the order to clear the spikes.

Selene responded without hesitation—Weezing was Taunted, so it could only attack.

Honchkrow whipped up a fierce wind with its wings—Defog swept across the field, clearing the Toxic Spikes.

But Weezing buzzed with static—Thunderbolt crackled and struck Honchkrow midair.

Electricity exploded around it—Thunderbolt was super effective against Flying-types.

Honchkrow cried out, flapping backward in pain.

Its defenses were low—while Weezing's Special Attack wasn't elite, the type advantage made it sting.

Honchkrow glared at Weezing, now furious.

"Again—Thunderbolt!" Selene ordered.

"Sucker Punch!"

Weezing charged electricity again, but Honchkrow's eyes flashed with black light—it dove forward like a blur.

Boom!

Sucker Punch landed before Thunderbolt could fire.

Weezing grunted and was knocked back. It fired anyway—but Honchkrow had already darted away.

"Psychic!"

Honchkrow spun midair, eyes glowing deep blue—Psychic-type: Psychic.

"Weezing!"

Selene's Pokémon floated higher, narrowly dodging the incoming wave of psychic energy.

"Don't let it go! Brave Bird!"

Honchkrow's wings ignited with red flames as it swooped toward Weezing—Flying-type: Brave Bird.

Aggressive and relentless!

Selene's brows knitted—Honchkrow's attacks were overwhelming, coming one after another.

There wasn't even time for Weezing to counter or defend.

BOOM!

The blazing red hawk slammed into Weezing, carrying it down in a spiraling dive.

The crash shook the field—dust exploded outward.

Honchkrow rose again, feathers crackling with red sparks—Brave Bird recoil damage.

"Weezing—Toxic!"

Selene suddenly shouted.

From within the dust cloud, a glob of deep-purple poison launched upward—Toxic hit!

It splattered against Honchkrow's chest and seeped in.

Hm?

Dylan's brows rose. Weezing was still down, unmoving.

But the smaller of its two heads looked fine.

Ah…

Selene was using the smaller head to attack, while the bigger one absorbed the damage.

Clever.

"So the smaller head can attack while the larger one tanks damage? What kind of setup is this?" Leaf blinked.

"This girl really is something," Lillie murmured.

The Toxic had hit. Honchkrow's chest glowed a sinister purple—badly poisoned.

The first tick wasn't too painful… but it'd get worse.

"Psychic!"

Dylan didn't want to wait around—Toxic would only hurt more over time.

Honchkrow's eyes gleamed again—waves of psychic energy shot toward the ground.

"Protect!"

Taunt had worn off—Weezing activated Protect just in time. The Psychic attack collided with the green shield.

As Protect faded, Honchkrow dived again.

Weezing had barely floated upright.

"Heat Wave!"

"Sucker Punch!"

Weezing spewed searing red flames—Fire-type: Heat Wave.

But Honchkrow swerved, dodging in a burst of speed enhanced by Sucker Punch, and appeared behind Weezing.

Its darkened wings slammed down, sending Weezing flying.

"Psychic!"

Dylan prepared to end it.

But then—the second tick of poison hit. Deep purple light burst across Honchkrow's body—it winced.

"T-Bolt!" Selene barked.

Weezing recovered midair—electricity blasted forth.

"Sky Attack!"

Honchkrow's body glowed blue—it barely dodged the Thunderbolt as its energy built up.

But—

Too close.

Selene's eyes widened. If Weezing were closer, Honchkrow wouldn't have had time to charge Sky Attack.

Boom!

Sky Attack slammed into Weezing again, smashing it into the ground.

CRASH!

Another plume of dust. Another impact crater.

"Psychic!"

Dylan wasn't giving Weezing any room to breathe. Sky Attack had no recoil—unlike Brave Bird.

Honchkrow's eyes glowed once more.

"Explosion!" Selene shouted.

Weezing's body flashed with blinding white light—like a small sun igniting on the field.

Art is an explosion!

Alarms blared in Honchkrow's instincts—it turned to flee, but—

Too late.

BOOOOM!

A colossal blast ripped across the arena. The shockwave shook the ground, blowing Dylan's and Selene's clothes back.

"Whoa! Straight to Explosion!"

"She had no choice. One more Psychic and Weezing was done anyway."

"No way Honchkrow survives that—it was already badly poisoned."

The Bird Keeper and friends muttered among themselves.

As the smoke cleared, a massive crater appeared on the battlefield.

At the center—Weezing lay unconscious.

Near the edge—Honchkrow was down, unmoving.

It had taken Thunderbolt, two rounds of Toxic, and the full force of Explosion.

Honchkrow's frail body couldn't take it.

"Weezing and Honchkrow are both unable to battle."

Leaf stepped in and declared the result.

Dylan returned Honchkrow, murmuring a few words of comfort.

Then he looked across at Selene, a flicker of approval in his eyes.

She'd taken down three of his Pokémon.

Even if two of them fell to Destiny Bond and Explosion—still, three.

(End of Chapter)

---

---

---

# 354: Chapter 355: Ultra Beast Kartana!

"No wonder she's Gym Leader Dylan's student—she's already taken out three of his Pokémon."

The older sister let out a heartfelt sigh. She'd been watching Rota Gym battles for a long time, and this was the first time she'd seen such a smooth opening.

Sure, Selene was technically behind, but given who she was fighting? Being in this kind of "disadvantage" against Dylan was already incredibly impressive.

"Right? Maybe she can even go for a four-kill," the Bird Keeper chimed in. Selene still had two Pokémon left—she might just pull it off.

But as for defeating Dylan? None of them were that optimistic.

Dylan's strength was well-known. Just a few days ago, he completely demolished Galar's undefeated Champion Leon.

They'd seen the video. Brutal.

On the field—

"Umbreon."

Dylan tossed out Umbreon again. The black-furred fox returned to the battlefield.

Umbreon—the ultimate lead. No matter what you were up against, it could always hold the line. Worst case, just pass the baton and get out.

And even Pokémon like Incineroar—Selene's go-to opener—couldn't do much to weaken it. Debuffs? Umbreon didn't care.

"Tch…" Selene clicked her tongue, frowning at the black fox.

After a second's thought, she threw out her sixth Poké Ball.

"Kartana, let's go!"

A flash of white—then a tiny, humanoid Pokémon floated onto the field.

It looked like a sword-wielding warrior—but if you looked closely, its entire body appeared to be made of folded paper. Its arms were long, razor-sharp blades.

The Ultra Beast codenamed UB-04: Blade—Kartana.

Beep~ Kartana. Grass/Steel-type. Drawn Sword Pokémon.

Ability: Beast Boost

Moves: Guillotine, Swords Dance, Sacred Sword, Leaf Blade, Night Slash, Detect, Aerial Ace…

Description: Said to have once sliced a massive metal tower in half with a single stroke. Classified as an Ultra Beast.

Kartana…

"Looks like Selene's going full offense now. Kartana's got insane attack power," Lillie said, eyes sparkling.

She knew Kartana well—her brother had one too.

It might look tiny, barely bigger than a hand, but its power was absurd.

She'd personally seen her brother's Kartana knock over a Tyranitar like it was nothing.

In the world of Pokémon, size meant nothing. And Kartana was proof—its Attack stat was practically legendary.

(Excluding Mythical and Legendary Pokémon, of course.)

The Pokédex said it could slice down steel towers in one stroke. Lillie didn't doubt that at all.

"Yeah, but Kartana's weaknesses are pretty obvious too."

Leaf nodded. Kartana had overwhelming physical offense, sure. But most Ultra Beasts were like that—extremely specialized.

Kartana had amazing Attack and solid Defense, plus decent Speed.

But its HP, Special Attack, and especially Special Defense? Trash-tier.

Not even worth trying to fix with training—it just was what it was.

That made it incredibly fragile against special moves.

A single Flamethrower from Charizard? Goodbye.

No exaggeration—Kartana's Fire-type weakness was 4x. It could melt in an instant.

"…Kartana." Dylan's eyes narrowed slightly.

"Curse."

Umbreon began chanting lowly, body glowing faintly—Curse activated.

Because Umbreon wasn't a Ghost-type, Curse had a different effect: it boosted Attack and Defense, but cut Speed.

"Sacred Sword!" Selene didn't hesitate.

Kartana let out a sharp cry and shot forward in a blur. Both arms lit with glowing blue energy, transforming into blades.

In just a breath or two, Kartana was in front of Umbreon—its right blade slashed down hard through the air.

Fighting-type: Sacred Sword!

Umbreon's Curse boost completed—it glowed briefly as its Attack and Defense rose, Speed dropped.

Slaaash!

Sacred Sword carved across Umbreon's body—Umbreon cried out in pain as the impact sent it flying.

Even with boosted Defense from Curse, Sacred Sword ignores defense changes.

And Fighting beats Dark—this hit hurt.

"Hard hitter," Dylan muttered.

His Umbreon had very high Defense—but it still took serious damage. That said it all.

"Don't let up! Keep using Sacred Sword!"

Kartana surged forward again—its left blade raised high, shining bright blue.

"Dig."

As Umbreon hit the ground, it immediately vanished—Dig sent it underground, leaving only a deep, dark hole.

"Force it out!"

Kartana floated upward, arms raised. The blades glowed, growing longer and sharper—Sacred Sword charged.

With a heavy slash, Kartana slammed both blades into the battlefield.

BOOM!

The ground trembled violently—deep fissures cracked open beneath the pressure.

Across the field, Umbreon surfaced—it had to. The underground quaking had become too intense to stay hidden.

"There!"

Kartana turned and shot toward Umbreon again.

"Baton Pass."

Dylan wasn't going to let Umbreon take another hit.

Just before Kartana reached it, Umbreon vanished into a streak of white light—Baton Pass activated.

Short exchange, but intense.

The crowd finally exhaled.

"Holy crap! That little thing's a beast!"

"That Sacred Sword was nuts! I've never seen it that massive before!"

"I swear, if that second strike had hit, Umbreon would've been done!"

"Selene's killing it!"

Excitement filled the gym—faces lit with shock, admiration, hype.

"That Kartana is really something," Dylan murmured. He truly respected its power.

"In that case…"

He threw out his fifth Poké Ball.

"Banette, you're up."

A small, purple puppet-like figure appeared—Banette.

Selene narrowed her eyes, already considering whether to pull Kartana out.

On paper, Banette couldn't compare to Kartana in raw power.

But battles were never about brute strength alone.

"Victory doesn't always go to the strongest," Dylan used to say—especially during his days leading Poison-type teams.

Selene, his "first disciple," had taken that lesson to heart.

"Shadow Sneak!" Dylan commanded.

Banette's form flickered into a ghostly blur—Ghost-type priority move. In an instant, it appeared in front of Kartana and slashed at it.

The sneak attack staggered Kartana. It crossed its sword arms in front of itself defensively.

"Return, Kartana!" Selene pulled out a Poké Ball.

"Mean Look!"

Too late.

A dark glimmer locked Kartana in place—the Mean Look landed.

A shadow clung to Kartana's feet, and the red recall beam from her Poké Ball fizzled out.

Selene's face froze.

Kartana was trapped.

"Selene, hesitation leads to chaos," Dylan said gently.

To be fair, even if she'd tried to switch the moment Banette appeared, it might not have helped.

With Prankster boosting status moves, Banette's Mean Look was just that fast.

"Leaf Blade!"

Selene gritted her teeth. She'd hesitated for just a second!

Whatever—it was just a Banette. Kartana was undefeated.

Green light flared from Kartana's blades—it lunged forward at high speed.

Grass-type: Leaf Blade!

It closed the gap fast—the two were already near each other. Kartana was like a flying sword, aiming to cut Banette clean through.

"Detect."

Dylan smiled faintly.

Banette's gem-like eyes shimmered—it bent backward at an impossible angle, just barely dodging the blow.

"Will-O-Wisp."

Banette's claws came together—blue ghostly flames floated toward Kartana.

Fire-type: Will-O-Wisp.

"Kartana!!" Selene cried.

Kartana slashed wildly—its blade-arms slicing the fireballs clean apart in midair.

"Tch… showoff."

Dylan clicked his tongue. Not just raw power—this thing had technique, too. Even ghost-fire couldn't land a hit.

But brute force alone wasn't always enough.

Banette's eyes gleamed with mischief—it clapped its little hands and cheered.

Eerie, whispery words floated through the air as it taunted and praised Kartana with mocking adoration.

Flatter.

The strange wave pulsed outward—Kartana's body shimmered as it absorbed the energy.

Its stance faltered slightly.

Its head twitched.

Then—it started spinning.

Confusion.

Flatter raised its Special Attack (useless for Kartana) but left it confused.

"…Kartana's confused now."

"Yikes. And that Banette has Fire Punch too."

"Who would've guessed a goofy Banette could neutralize something as scary as Kartana?"

The Bird Keeper trio sighed. This was the same Kartana that made Sacred Sword cause earthquakes.

And now it was stumbling around like a drunk, thanks to that weird little puppet.

"…."

Selene stared at her Pokémon, watching it stumble in a daze like it was shadowboxing the air.

Her expression twitched.

And she began to think:

…Okay. Now what?

(End of Chapter)

---

# 355: Chapter 356: Look Down on Us!

What should she do?

There didn't seem to be much of an option—Selene could only try to snap Kartana out of its confusion.

Normally, a confused Pokémon could just be recalled to its Poké Ball and given time to calm down. But with Mean Look still in effect, Kartana couldn't be withdrawn.

That made things tricky.

"Fire Punch."

Dylan didn't hold back. He immediately ordered Sableye to attack.

Flames ignited around Sableye's claw as it charged toward Kartana—not moving too fast, holding back a little in case something unexpected happened.

Confusion wasn't like sleep—the opponent didn't become defenseless. You never knew what they might do next.

"Kartana! Leaf Blade!"

Selene called out, unwilling to give up. She couldn't just surrender like this!

Kartana continued stumbling in dizzy circles, not even showing any signs of having heard her.

Sableye leapt high—Fire Punch slammed down hard on Kartana's tiny body.

BOOM!

Flames burst out, and Kartana let out a sharp cry as it crashed into the ground.

A Fire-type move, and Kartana was 4x weak to Fire.

Still—it wasn't enough for a one-hit KO. Sableye didn't have high Attack, and Kartana's physical Defense was top-tier.

"…It's no use. Trying to make a confused Pokémon obey commands is too difficult."

Yellow sighed.

To be fair, Kartana's current dizzy stumbling was already tame. Some confused Pokémon thrashed wildly, even hurt themselves.

Like right now.

BANG! BANG! BANG!

Thudding noises came from the battlefield—Kartana was smashing its head into the ground repeatedly.

Yellow facepalmed. Yeah. Like this.

"Kartana! Wake up already!"

Selene looked miserable, practically tearing her hair out.

"Fire Punch again," Dylan said with a chuckle. He was enjoying this a little too much.

Sableye's small legs carried it forward once more, paw wreathed in flames.

"Kartana!"

As Sableye got close—maybe it was Selene's desperate yelling, maybe sheer luck—but Kartana suddenly stopped mid-head-smash.

Its previously blank eyes flashed with clarity. The blade-like arms lit up with sharp green light—Leaf Blade!

CLANG!

Sableye's Fire Punch struck the glowing blade—but Kartana's arm slashed right through the flames, dispersing them and slicing toward Sableye.

Sableye had been cautious, keeping its guard up. It instantly sprang backward, but not fast enough—Kartana's blade grazed its chest, drawing sparks and a pained grunt.

That hurt!

It didn't land fully, but if it had, the hit would've been devastating.

The power gap between them was crystal clear.

"Nice one, Kartana!!" Selene beamed.

But a second later—her expression froze.

Kartana had gone back to holding its head and groaning in confusion. Then, with a thud, it started banging its head against the ground again.

It wasn't fully snapped out of it.

"Fire Punch."

Dylan's lips curled upward. A moment of clarity in confusion didn't mean the effect was over.

Sableye dashed in once again, flames dancing on its claw.

"Kartana! Use Leaf Blade again!"

Selene pleaded with hope.

…But this time?

Nothing.

Even as Sableye closed in, even as the burning fist descended—Kartana just kept slamming its head into the dirt.

BOOM!

The flames erupted once more, and Kartana collapsed. It had taken too much damage—twice from Fire Punch, plus the self-inflicted hits. It was done.

"Kartana is unable to battle. Sableye wins."

Leaf stepped forward and announced the result.

"God, Sableye is so annoying," Selene muttered, recalling her fainted Kartana with a frustrated huff.

She shot a dark look at the alien-looking puppet.

Ugh! When I go to Hoenn, I'm totally catching one of those creepy little jerks.

But now—she only had one Pokémon left.

Selene looked down at her final Poké Ball, then glanced across at Dylan.

Without realizing it… she'd been pushed to the edge.

"Let me see your final strength, Selene."

Dylan smiled warmly, raising a hand in anticipation.

"Oh, you will!"

Selene's eyes curved into crescents—her pure, confident smile returning.

"Incineroar! Let's do this!"

She threw her last Poké Ball high. A burst of white light—and with a mighty roar, Incineroar landed on the field, arms wide and teeth bared at Sableye.

Selene's very first partner—her ace.

"Let's give it our all, buddy! We're not letting Big Bro Gym Leader look down on us!"

Selene clutched her floral sun hat tightly, burning with fighting spirit.

"ROAR!!"

Incineroar let out a deafening roar—its fierce presence filled the arena.

Intimidate activated!

A ripple of white energy spread from Sableye's body—its Attack was reduced by one stage.

"So full of spirit," Dylan said with a chuckle.

In truth, Sableye was at a big disadvantage here.

Its Prankster-boosted status moves were useless against Dark-types—like Incineroar.

"Flatter!" Dylan still gave the command.

Even without Prankster's priority, the move could still be used.

"Fake Out!" Selene countered instantly.

It wouldn't deal damage to Sableye, but that wasn't the point—she just wanted the priority from Fake Out to get in close and avoid Flatter.

Sableye clapped its hands and cheered loudly, voice filled with exaggerated praise.

But Incineroar didn't flinch. It zipped across the field in a red blur—its Fake Out allowing it to instantly close the distance.

In a flash, Incineroar was behind Sableye.

"Taunt!"

Hands on its hips, Incineroar let out a savage roar straight into Sableye's face.

The ripple of Taunt surged forward—Sableye staggered, eyes narrowing.

"Nice!" Selene grinned. That took care of Sableye's status moves.

"Keep your distance. Use Power Gem!" Dylan decided not to brute force it.

This Incineroar was strong—his student's first Pokémon, her ace. Head-on wasn't wise.

Sableye jumped back—gemstones formed around it and launched forward in a barrage of glowing beams.

"Darkest Lariat!"

Incineroar spun into a fiery tornado—arms out, spinning like a drill.

The rock blasts from Power Gem shattered against the spinning attack.

Incineroar closed in, then—

BOOM!

The spinning blow slammed into Sableye, launching it across the field.

Dark-type: Darkest Lariat!

Being a Ghost-type, Sableye had no resistance to this hit—it landed hard.

Sableye tumbled, then rose with a low growl—its jewel-like eyes glaring at Incineroar.

"Flamethrower!"

Incineroar roared—its belt of flames ignited, spewing a stream of fire straight at Sableye.

"Dodge!"

Sableye sidestepped—but Incineroar sprinted after it, fire still spewing.

Sableye leapt into the air to avoid the flames.

"Throat Chop!"

Black energy surged around Incineroar's claws—it leapt up and struck.

Dark-type: Throat Chop!

"Dodge it!"

Sableye twisted midair—its body bent unnaturally. It managed to barely dodge.

Just barely.

"Power Gem!"

"Darkest Lariat!"

The two Pokémon clashed again—Sableye unleashed a burst of gem-light, while Incineroar spun up to intercept.

BOOM!

Both moves hit—Incineroar winced from the hit, but its spinning arms crashed into Sableye again.

Sableye hit the ground hard. Its defenses were mediocre—two direct hits from Darkest Lariat had left it reeling.

But Power Gem had landed as well—and Rock beats Fire. Even with its modest Special Attack, Sableye had dealt a decent blow.

"Finish it, Incineroar!" Selene's fist pumped skyward, her momentum peaking.

Taunt wouldn't last forever. Once it wore off, Sableye could stall with Pain Split or other tricks.

Selene wasn't giving it that chance.

"Blast Burn!"

ROAR!!

Incineroar's entire body flared with intense red energy—it slammed its fists into the ground.

Cracks spread across the arena—glowing crimson lines racing beneath Sableye.

BOOOOM!!

It was like a volcanic eruption—Blast Burn burst from the ground, engulfing Sableye in a towering inferno.

A Fire-type signature move, known only to Fire-type starters and Mew.

The entire arena was bathed in red light—fans gasped as their eyes reflected the flames.

One second… Two… Three…

The inferno slowly faded. The ground was scorched black—Sableye lay at the center, knocked out.

Not far away, Incineroar stood frozen—its body locked in place from Blast Burn's post-move recoil.

"Sableye is unable to battle. Incineroar wins."

Leaf announced the result.

"Wow! Selene's Incineroar is amazing!" Yellow exclaimed, clapping her hands to her chest, eyes sparkling.

Super strong! It totally dominated Sableye.

"Of course! That's the very first Pokémon Big Bro Gym Leader gave Selene—her absolute favorite," Asha said proudly, beaming with pride.

She couldn't help it—Selene was her bestie.

"Big Sis is really strong," Silver nodded seriously.

Watching the full battle so far, he had to admit Selene was powerful.

Taking the title of Dylan's top student… might take longer than he thought.

"…She's come a long way. Impressive."

Dylan recalled his fainted Sableye, smiling at Incineroar.

The tiger puffed its chest out proudly—glowing with satisfaction.

Dylan chuckled.

Despite its fierce, savage look, this Incineroar and his own Incineroar were actually twin sisters.

Yep—two big scary girl cats.

(End of Chapter)

---

# 356: Chapter 357: Want to Try Challenging a Legend?

"Sending you out on a journey really was the right choice. Every step along the way has clearly settled into your eyes now."

Dylan's voice was filled with admiration. After all, just half a year ago, this girl had barely even qualified as a Trainer.

And now—she was standing on the battlefield opposite him, fighting with everything she had.

It felt surreal, but not unpleasant.

"Your Incineroar is impressive, Selene. As it happens, I recently obtained a new Dark-type Legendary Pokémon in Galar. Its strength is just about right to be your opponent. Want to give it a try?"

Dylan considered it. He had originally planned to send out Kingambit to counter Incineroar—but on second thought, this seemed like a much better choice.

"Of course I want to!" Selene's eyes lit up, her voice bursting with excitement.

A Dark-type Legendary Pokémon?! How could she possibly turn that down?

Besides, if her big bro Gym Leader said it was a good match for her Incineroar, she believed him. Dylan always ran his gym battles fairly—he never used Pokémon that far outclassed the challenger's.

Well… except for the Golden Legendary team. That was the one and only exception.

Selene's eyes burned with anticipation. Challenging a Legendary—that was the dream of every Trainer.

She couldn't be more excited.

"That's my apprentice." Dylan smiled, clearly satisfied with her answer.

"Whoa—is she really about to battle one of Dylan's Legendaries?" the older girl in the stands exclaimed, fired up.

Legendary Pokémon had long been one of Dylan's signature aces.

"Selene's got guts," the bird tamer added with a whistle.

"Legendary or not, it's still a Pokémon," the psychic said flatly, unimpressed. "Strong, sure—but not invincible."

The title might be intimidating, but as a Trainer, when you're offered the chance to challenge a Legendary, how could you say no?

And Dylan had made it clear: this one wasn't leagues above Selene in strength.

If it were him down there, no way he'd pass it up either.

"Word is, Brandon's been challenged multiple times this year alone," the psychic added.

Brandon was known for having a team of legendary titans—widely known—and second only to Dylan in terms of fame among Legendary trainers.

From what the psychic had heard, Brandon had even lost to a few challengers this year.

On the field—

"Then let's begin..."

Dylan pulled out a deep crimson Poké Ball and tossed it behind him with a flick.

"Moltres, battle ready!"

SCREEEEE—!

A strange, almost sinister cry rang out as a massive black-and-red bird spread its wings, flames rippling like ribbons from its feathers.

"What the—Moltres?!"

Shock rippled through the crowd as they stared at the majestic figure.

Wait. Was that really… Moltres?

The name didn't seem to fit.

This Moltres looked nothing like the fiery bird from the Kanto legends.

Its entire body was cloaked in black plumage, a crimson belly glowing with eerie energy. Its red beak curved like a scythe, and its wings and tail shimmered with a fire-like aura that seemed more spectral than hot.

The vibe was dark. Wicked. Wrong.

This wasn't the Moltres they knew.

The Kanto Moltres was famous—even if most people hadn't seen it in person, the sacred flame it provided for the Indigo League torch relay was iconic.

Images of Moltres were everywhere online… but this? This was an entirely different bird.

Even calling it a shiny form felt off. It was clearly a different species altogether.

Ding~ Moltres (Galarian Form). Dark/Flying-type. The Wicked Flame Pokémon.

Ability: Berserk

Moves: Brave Bird, Hurricane, Fiery Wrath, Air Slash, Sucker Punch, Payback...

Description: Those who come into contact with the malevolent flame-like aura surrounding it will find their soul scorched to ash. (Galarian Form)

"...Okay, but why is this thing still called 'Moltres' when it's not even a Fire-type?" Leaf grumbled, reading off the Rotomdex's data.

Seriously. This was nonsense.

Just because it looked like it was on fire didn't mean it was.

And it wasn't. It had no Fire-type moves other than, like, Sunny Day…

"I don't know either, Rotom..." the Rotomdex flailed helplessly.

"Galarian Moltres. Dark/Flying. Selene, let's see if you can take it down."

Dylan raised a hand, smiling as he issued the challenge.

He hadn't had this bird for long—just recently caught it in Galar. To be honest, he hadn't trained it up that much either.

By his estimation, this Galarian Moltres was around the same level as Selene's Incineroar.

It was a standard-tier Legendary—rare, sure, with superior stats, but not overwhelmingly overpowered.

"Yes, sir!" Selene locked eyes on the ominous bird. Though it didn't look anything like the Moltres she expected, her fighting spirit blazed even hotter.

This was her first-ever battle against a Legendary Pokémon!

"Incineroar! Flamethrower!"

Selene threw her arm forward with confidence, striking first.

She wasn't sure why a Moltres didn't have Fire typing—but that just made things easier.

Dark resisted Dark, but her Fire-type moves would hit for neutral damage. That was fine.

Incineroar bellowed—its waist flames roared into a pillar, spewing a column of fire at Galarian Moltres.

"Fiery Wrath," Dylan said coolly.

Moltres raised its head and let out an eerie shriek—its wings flared open, black-and-red energy swirling ominously around it.

Fiery Wrath—a Dark-type special move, exclusive to Galarian Moltres.

It surged forward, colliding mid-air with Flamethrower in a swirling clash of black flames and red-orange fire.

The clash didn't last long—Fiery Wrath overwhelmed Flamethrower and pushed through.

"Incineroar!" Selene's eyes narrowed.

Sensing the danger, Incineroar cut off its flames and dove aside just in time.

BOOM!Fiery Wrath crashed where it had just stood—flames erupted violently.

"Strong Special Attack. We can't outblast it with ranged moves."

Selene instantly reassessed.

This Moltres was clearly a special attacker—trading firepower with it was a losing battle.

Worse, it was flying. Incineroar had no way to reach it unless it came down on its own.

Unless Moltres decided to get close—how could she fight it?

"Flame Charge!"

Selene quickly found her solution.

Incineroar roared and charged, flames licking at its fur—Flame Charge boosted its speed with every hit.

Yup—this was the plan. Stack up speed, and even if she couldn't fly, she could outpace Moltres and strike when it landed.

All martial arts under heaven bow to speed!

"Sucker Punch."

Dylan smiled—he saw through her plan at once.

Galarian Moltres's eyes flashed red—it dove like a shadow, striking before Flame Charge could finish.

WHAM!

The sucker punch collided hard with Incineroar, staggering it and pushing it back.

"Thunder Punch!"

Selene seized the opening.

Incineroar charged—lightning surging along its right paw.

Moltres beat its wings furiously, trying to rise—but Incineroar pushed through the wind, arm cocked back.

BOOM!Thunder Punch struck home—right in Moltres's gut. Sparks flew.

Electric beats Flying. The hit landed cleanly.

"Hurricane!"

Fury sparked in Moltres's glowing blue eyes—it beat its wings, summoning a swirling cyclone.

Flying-type: Hurricane!

The wind roared—Incineroar was blasted into the air, howling in pain.

Not good!

Selene's heart dropped.

Thunder Punch had hit, but Moltres countered way too fast. Its defense was no joke.

"Brave Bird!"

SCREEE!

Moltres soared upward—its body wreathed in a brilliant blue flame, forming the shape of a divine bird.

Brave Bird incoming.

It locked onto the falling Incineroar.

"Protect!"

Selene reacted instantly.

Incineroar raised a green barrier midair—barely.

BOOM!Brave Bird collided with Protect—the barrier held, but Incineroar still plummeted.

"Ancient Power!"

As the shield vanished, Moltres flared its wings—summoning glowing stones that crashed down.

Rock-type: Ancient Power!

BOOM BOOM BOOM!Stones slammed down just as Incineroar hit the ground—dust exploded upward.

Incineroar roared in pain—Rock was strong against Fire-types.

"Hurricane!" Dylan pressed on.

Moltres flapped again—another cyclone barreled down.

"Explosive Blaze!"

ROAR!!

Incineroar's furious bellow echoed—flames surged skyward in a massive eruption.

The attack intercepted the oncoming Hurricane—Moltres couldn't dodge.

It squawked in pain, retreating as the flames licked at its feathers.

Still—the blowback only seemed to fuel its rage.

Galarian Moltres's Berserk ability triggered—its Special Attack rose sharply.

The storm couldn't break through the flames—Moltres couldn't follow up for now.

But that was fine.

Dylan watched as the fire cleared, the glowing red aura intensifying around his Legendary bird.

This one had a real temper.

No wonder its Ability was Berserk, and its signature move was Fiery Wrath.

He recalled—when he brought this Moltres home, it clashed constantly with the Kanto Moltres.

Totally different personalities.

Galarian Moltres looked down on Kantonian Moltres for being weak.

Kanto Moltres dismissed Galarian Moltres as some weird mutant.

He had to separate them constantly to keep them from fighting.

Come to think of it… Zapdos and Articuno had the same issues.

(End of Chapter)

# 357: Chapter 358: For the Sake of the League!

Though the explosive blaze had temporarily forced Galarian Moltres back and disrupted its Hurricane, it ultimately didn't do much to change the outcome.

"Ancient Power."

Dylan gave the next command.

As Galarian Moltres flapped its wings, glowing boulders formed around it and began to rain down on the Incineroar below.

Still stunned from the recoil of its own Blast Burn, Incineroar couldn't dodge—its eyes widened as the rocks crashed down and buried it with a pained roar.

A bright white glow rippled over Moltres's body—Ancient Power's secondary effect activated, boosting its Attack, Defense, Special Attack, Special Defense, and Speed, all by one stage!

"Tch..." Selene clicked her tongue. This fight was already difficult, and now Moltres had gotten buffed? Great.

Under Dylan's calm orders, another Ancient Power followed, and Incineroar was buried again. When the dust settled, it was unconscious.

"Incineroar is unable to battle. Galarian Moltres wins!"

"Challenger Selene's six Pokémon are all unable to battle. The Rota Gym challenge ends in failure."

Leaf announced the final result.

It was over. Incineroar had been Selene's last Pokémon. Its defeat meant the battle—and the challenge—was lost.

"Aww, what a shame."

Selene didn't look too upset though—after all, defeating her teacher wasn't going to be easy.

Sure, she'd dared to hope.

If she'd actually won, she'd have been the first ever to earn a Rota Badge by beating Dylan in battle.

As the audience buzzed, Dylan stepped forward to meet her.

"You've grown a lot stronger. You didn't let me down."

Seeing the hopeful sparkle in her eyes, Dylan didn't hold back his praise.

"Hehe~" Selene beamed, squinting with pride.

"Keep it up. Next year, maybe go for the Kanto Champion title."

"Eh? You mean I'd have to beat you to do that?"

"If you're up for it, I won't stop you."

Dylan chuckled. Truth was, he was planning to retire from the Champion position next year anyway—but if Selene insisted, he wouldn't mind sticking around a little longer.

"Hmph~ Do what you want, Gym Leader Big Bro~" she giggled. She understood his implication.

Challenging Dylan the Gym Leader? Fun.Challenging Dylan the Kanto Champion? A death wish.

Hadn't she seen what he did to Leon?

"You little brat..." Dylan shook his head, amused. Selene really was sharp.

"You were excellent today. Here—this badge is yours."

He pulled out an ornate velvet box and opened it to reveal the sleek, black Dark-type Rota Badge.

"Thankies, Gym Leader Big Bro~!"

Selene snatched it up without hesitation, absolutely glowing with joy. Her voice even turned syrupy sweet.

"Yes! I—this lady—finally got my Rota Badge!"

She held it up, admiring the fine craftsmanship. Sure, she didn't beat Dylan to earn it outright…

But who cared? As long as it was a badge Dylan gave her, she didn't mind one bit!

"Oh, by the way, if I happen to lose it by accident… you won't be mad, right?" she asked with a big innocent smile, blinking at him.

"Of course not. It's yours now. Do what you want with it," Dylan said, eyeing her suspiciously. He knew that look all too well.

"Ehehe~ But I won't lose it! I'm gonna make it into a necklace and wear it every day!"

"Your badges are worth a lot these days, you know."

She kept flipping the badge over, admiring it. So shiny, so rare!

Selene knew that on the black market, Rota Badges were selling for insane amounts.

Normally, a Rota elemental badge went for over 10 million. But now? Wealthy collectors were offering 20 million to buy just one.

Not that anyone was selling. No one was stupid enough to part with something so hard to earn.

The number of people Dylan had given badges to was tiny—and they were all elite Trainers. Even the "weakest" recipients were people like Lijia, an official Gym Leader.

They weren't exactly hurting for cash.

"Yeah, well, I set the bar pretty high," Dylan shrugged.

His standards for badge distribution were notoriously strict. But now that he was the Kanto Champion, who could argue?

Anyone who wanted recognition from him had better earn it.

"Now then, let's talk about what you could've done better during our battle."

Dylan returned Galarian Moltres to its ball and looked at her with a serious expression.

Selene's eyes sparkled—this was the best part. When Dylan gave her feedback, it meant he cared.

…

Far away, in the Johto region's Mahogany Town—the northernmost settlement and home to an air of quiet suspicion.

Known as the Village of Ninjas, it was ironically quite peaceful.

Its Ice-type Gym, led by the aging Pryce, was one of the more respected in the region.

Within the gym, cold mist hung in the air, a silent frost coating everything. Dozens of beautiful, lifelike ice sculptures lined the walls.

On his wheelchair, Pryce stared at a screen.

"Dialga..."

He murmured the name of the Temporal Pokémon, eyes reflecting the flickering footage on screen.

He hadn't cared much at first. But that morning, hearing townsfolk talk about "Time" and "Dialga" had caught his interest.

A Legendary that surpassed even Celebi in controlling time.

If he could gain its help…

He gently stroked the Swinub on his lap, mind spinning with possibilities.

He didn't want power. All he wanted… was to go back and fix a single tragedy.

To let Lapras reunite with its parents.

"No matter what…"

His voice was low, his resolve chilling.

…

Two days later, the Indigo Plateau's prestigious Indigo League Tournament came to a dramatic close.

And the winner was… Jeanette from Crimson City.

When Dylan heard that, he was genuinely surprised.

He'd always thought she had talent—she'd chosen the perfect type specialization. But to take the championship? He hadn't seen that coming.

The final match was between her and Gary. She barely clinched the win.

Ash had lost in the semifinals—to Jeanette as well.

Which had also surprised Dylan. Ash's Charizard wasn't nearly as disobedient as it used to be—his overall team was stronger than in the anime.

But the rest of Ash's Pokémon were still weak. Charizard had dragged the whole team to the semifinals.

And this year's tournament quality? Thanks to Dylan's influence, way higher than the original "baby cup" standard.

"Gary, you moron. I told him to prep for Poison-types," grumbled Blue from his Elite Four office.

If it had been him, he could've taken Jeanette down with just one of his main team.

Still… his kid brother was the runner-up. Red's brother only made it to Top 4.

So yeah. He felt better.

"The Indigo League's done. Why are we even talking about this?" Dylan chuckled as he scrolled through forums.

"Let's focus on the real issue," he said to Chairman Goodshow.

They'd all been called together for a reason—the full Kanto Elite Four was assembled, even Giovanni.

"Our intel says that Lance and his crew are currently on an island called Suo." Goodshow's voice was heavy.

"Former Champions, huh? What's up with them?" Blue asked lazily, arms crossed.

Red stayed silent. Leaf filed her nails. Giovanni sipped tea. Dylan kept scrolling.

Goodshow frowned. Were they always this relaxed? But… at least they were competent.

"This is serious. Please pay attention."

"Yes, sir, go ahead."

"Lance has been recognized by the Ideal Dragon God. His goal… is to purge the world of all evil."

"That sounds... noble?" Blue blinked.

He probably meant criminals and Pokémon hunters, right? Who would object to that?

"There's a problem with the man holding that ideal," Giovanni said flatly.

"Exactly," Goodshow nodded. "His heart was in the right place. But he's gone too far."

"In Lance's eyes, evil is absolute. He believes even those who commit crimes out of desperation should be erased."

"He's willing to sacrifice innocents if that's what it takes to eliminate 'evil.'"

Goodshow rubbed his temples, exhausted.

Blue looked thoughtful. Red's eyes flickered.

"The Pokémon League exists to foster peace and harmony between humans and Pokémon. Life only comes once. People may stray, but they can still choose to atone," Goodshow said solemnly.

If someone committed unforgivable crimes, fine—justice would be served.

"But Chairman," Blue muttered, "you're too soft."

"Let me ask you this: a man who gave to charity for sixty years… one day, starving, he steals a loaf of bread. Is he evil?"

Blue hesitated.

"The answer is yes. But we would also offer him support and fairness," Goodshow said firmly.

"Lance's logic is flawed. Mass killings won't bring a better future. That's why we—the Pokémon League—must stop him."

"I'm asking you, the current Kanto Elite Four, to defeat him. Show him what true justice means."

The room went quiet.

Then—

"Hell yeah! We get to beat up our predecessors? Say no more." Blue's eyes gleamed.

All that moral philosophy was a bore—but this? This he could get behind.

"That's right. I'm justice! Anyone who opposes me is evil!"

"For the sake of the League and Kanto, we will not back down!"

Giovanni adjusted his dark suit, a serene smile on his face.

"Chairman, don't worry. As the head of the Kanto Elite Four, I—Giovanni—will bring Lance to justice."

No ulterior motives at all. No personal vendettas. Just pure, selfless loyalty to the League.

Goodshow smiled, proud and relieved.

(End of Chapter)

---

---

# 358: Chapter 359: Let’s Go!

Pfft~Hearing Giovanni speak so righteously, Leaf nearly burst out laughing.

Like, seriously—who else could understand this better?

The former Rocket Boss, now not only the head of Kanto's Elite Four but also declaring in the Indigo Plateau League HQ, right in the Elite Four's office, that he was going to take down former Kanto Champion and Elite Four leader Lance.

And he said it with such conviction too!Leaf almost ruined her freshly done manicure trying to hold it in.

"Nice! That's the spirit, Giovanni-senpai. Yep, a bunch of ex-Elites? Please, we can take 'em no problem!"

Blue was hyped. He'd been bored out of his mind lately. Being a Kanto Elite Four member didn't feel all that different from before—he barely had anything to do.

And those annoying online trolls questioning his skills? Hah! If they had the guts, they should just come challenge him directly!

Saying he was weaker than the previous Elite Four? What a joke!

"Very good, but hold off on the excitement—sit down for now."

Chairman Goodshow smiled, heartened by the youthful fire. This—this was the future of the Kanto League.

"According to inside sources, Lance and the others are still on Cerise Island. They're planning to use the island's special nature to summon a legendary bird Pokémon."

Goodshow's gray eyebrows knit together once more.

"In Kanto, that would mean either Ho-Oh or Lugia, right?" Giovanni mused.

"Correct. Lance already has Zekrom. If he gets Ho-Oh or Lugia too… we'll have a serious problem."

Goodshow sounded troubled. He knew just how strong Lance was.

Lance had always been one of the top Champions of his era. His dragon legion was already formidable, and with Zekrom? His strength had only soared.

Add Ho-Oh or Lugia on top of that? Disaster.

Giovanni's eyes flickered. If that happened, not even he might be able to stop Lance.

"Tch… two Legendary Pokémon, huh." Blue scratched his head, clearly uneasy.

"And then there's Agatha and Lorelei too," Goodshow added heavily.

Well, Agatha and Lorelei anyway—he was pretty sure Bruno had already defected to Dylan's side.

Good ol' Bruno. Loyal and dependable.

"Still, we have Dylan," Leaf smiled breezily.

"Isn't it kinda unfair if Dylan gets involved though? Honestly, I think he only needs to deal with Zekrom and the bird—leave the rest to us," Blue suggested.

"Dylan's the Champion. He shouldn't have to step in directly."

What he meant was—he wanted the glory. If Dylan fought, what was the point of the rest of them going?

Dylan alone could probably wipe out the whole enemy squad.

"You're so considerate, Blue," Dylan said, side-eyeing him.

"No need to thank me. Just sit tight on Reshiram while we win this for you," Blue said with a grand flourish.

"There are four of them, and four of us. Perfect match-up."

In Blue's mind, as the most handsome of the current Elite Four, it was only right that he got to fight Lance himself. He'd been dying to take on that legendary dragon team!

"Chairman, where's Cerise Island?" he asked.

"I'll mark it on the map for you. You must stop them before they complete the summoning."

Goodshow sighed.

"Once they summon the bird, Lance plans to launch an attack on Vermilion City. It's tragic…"

"Wait—attack it?" Blue blinked.

"Literally. He's targeting Lt. Surge," Giovanni quickly realized.

"Ohhh, right. Surge used to be part of Team Rocket." Blue caught on and clicked his tongue.

Well, that explains it.

"Surge has turned over a new leaf. I personally persuaded him," Goodshow said, rubbing his temples. "But Lance won't accept that—he sees Surge as a former high-ranking criminal."

In Lance's eyes, people like Surge didn't deserve redemption—they deserved eradication.

"Alright, gentlemen. Rest well and prepare yourselves. You depart tomorrow."

"Go forth—and return victorious!"

Goodshow smiled warmly at the current Kanto Champion and Elite Four.

After the meeting, Blue and Red went off to the training grounds.

Dylan, Leaf, and Giovanni returned to their respective gyms.

Reshiram still stood proud at the League HQ's front plaza. As Dylan's most famous steed, the White Dragon was something of a celebrity in Kanto.

As the three exited the building, two Officer Jennys were saluting on either side of Reshiram.

The majestic white wings were folded gracefully, its twin tails swaying gently. It was beautiful and sacred.

Another Jenny was taking pictures—Reshiram had basically become a tourist attraction.

Dylan couldn't help but chuckle. He'd left Reshiram out to enjoy the sun, not to become a photo op.

"Champion Dylan! Elite Giovanni, Elite Leaf!"

The three Jennys greeted them quickly.

"Hello," Dylan replied with a gentle smile.

"So tomorrow's the big battle with Lance and the others, huh? The world really is strange."

Watching the Jennys walk off, Giovanni couldn't help but reflect on the absurdity of life.

Once, he was the Rocket Boss, working tirelessly for the criminal syndicate. The moment his identity was revealed, the world turned on him.

Now? He was Kanto's head Elite Four, and people addressed him respectfully everywhere as "Elite Giovanni."

And tomorrow… he'd be fighting Lance for the good of the League.

It was surreal. So surreal that even Giovanni couldn't fully put it into words.

Leaf blinked at Giovanni.

"Feeling confident about defeating Lance 'for justice' tomorrow?" Dylan teased.

"No confidence? Gotta find some. This may be my one and only chance," Giovanni said with a cryptic smile.

Indeed—it might be the only time he could legally call Lance 'evil' and publicly blast him for it.

"If Blue handles Zekrom and the bird, I can deal with the rest," Giovanni added.

"But can you guys handle Lance?"

Dylan tilted his head. He really hoped Lance could be convinced to change. But to make that happen, Lance needed a wake-up call—a complete ideological breakdown.

He needed to lose.

"If I could use my ace, I'd be confident. But I won't be fighting alone."

Giovanni paused. The issue was, he couldn't reveal his true ace: his mega Beedrill. It was too unique. If he used it, the world would immediately realize who he used to be.

He had to stick with his Ground-types. That made beating Lance harder, but hey—he wasn't alone.

He had Blue, Red, and Dylan—all fighting for justice to bring down the wicked Lance.

"Good then. See you tomorrow."

After bidding Giovanni farewell, Dylan and Leaf hopped onto Reshiram and flew back to Rota City.

Once back at Rota Gym, Dylan made a call.

"Yo, Gym Leader Bro?"

"Selene, where are you?"

"I'm at Mt. Moon. Hey, Hiker dude, don't leave! Fight me again! I'm not gonna rob you!"

Dylan chuckled. He could imagine the chaos on her end.

"Ran off again… sigh. Gym Leader Bro, why are they all so chicken? I'm just a cute little girl!"

"…"

Dylan was silent.

"Anyway, got time tomorrow? I'm taking you on a raid."

"Huh?" Selene blinked. What now?

The Next Day – Indigo Plateau

"So this is your student, Dylan?" Blue eyed Selene with interest.

He'd seen her before, during his own Rota Gym challenge.

"That's right. Selene's gotten pretty strong. I thought I'd let her see some real action."

Dylan grinned. If nothing else, he was a responsible mentor.

He'd thought about bringing Yellow too—but decided against it.

"Oh? I'll have to test her out sometime," Blue said, raising a brow.

Anyone Dylan praised that much had to be impressive. He made a mental note to challenge her later—just a friendly spar between seniors and juniors.

"Let's move."

Dylan nodded to Goodshow, then boarded Reshiram with Leaf and Selene.

Blue and Red took to the skies on Pidgeot and Charizard, while Giovanni rode a Salamence borrowed from Dylan.

Together, they soared toward Cerise Island—to challenge the former Kanto Elite Four!

"Lance, place the Green Badge."

Agatha spoke with eerie calm. Around the volcano's rim, seven of the Kanto starter badges had already been slotted in.

Only the final one remained.

Lance, atop Aerodactyl, glided to the volcano's mouth and dropped the Green Badge.

In that instant, the skies began to shift.

Dark clouds swirled and layered across the heavens.

The volcano trembled violently. A column of energy shot straight up into the sky.

"It's happening!"

Agatha, Lorelei, and Bruno stood on a nearby cliff, their hair and robes whipping in the wind, eyes locked on the sky above.

Dark clouds twisted into a massive vortex. From its heart… a colossal figure emerged.

"There it is. The Legendary Bird Pokémon…"

Lorelei pushed her glasses up, staring intently.

A deep, haunting cry echoed across the land.

From the swirling storm descended a great white bird, wings spread wide.

"Lugia!"

(End of Chapter)

# 359: Chapter 360: Just Washed-Up Relics

Reshiram landed, and Dylan and the others dismounted from their Pokémon.

"So this is it? Cerise Island."

Blue took off his sunglasses, eyes fixed on the massive dormant volcano in the center of the island. No doubt about it—it matched all the intel.

"Is it gonna rain? …No, something's off."

Blue glanced at the sky. Thick black clouds were gathering overhead, heavy with menace. It looked like a storm was brewing—but something didn't feel right.

Giovanni turned to look behind them. The dark clouds stretched endlessly across the sky, but it had been bright and sunny during their flight over. Something was definitely wrong.

"When the heavens shift strangely… a great evil stirs!"

Selene's expression was serious—but the next second…

"Ow! That hurts!"

She clutched her head, eyes teary after taking a hard flick to the forehead. Dylan calmly retracted his "offending" right hand, expression blank.

"Cut back on those fantasy novels," Dylan said with a glare.

Selene shrank back, intimidated by her master's authority.

"Alright, fellas, let me say a few words."

Dylan clapped his hands, drawing the attention of Giovanni and the others.

"We all know why we're here. The former Elite Four have strayed from the path. As the upright current Elite Four, it's our duty to guide them back."

He coughed and straightened up, sounding official.

"…That's the PR version. Let's get to the real plan."

"No need to make it fancy. We'll split into four groups and advance from all sides toward the center."

"If you run into one of the former Elite Four, don't wait for explanations—just take them down. We'll contact each other afterward."

Dylan lifted his communicator and waved it around.

"If you encounter Lance, call me. I doubt you can handle both of his Legendaries."

"Go all-out—I've got your backs."

"I've already promised Chairman Goodshow to have this wrapped up by 5:00. You get the idea."

Dylan glanced at his phone. It was just after 1 PM—four hours left.

"No problem! Just some washed-up exes. Leave it to me," Blue said, smug and confident. He turned on his heel and strode toward the right path with flair.

As long as he didn't run into Lance, he was confident in beating any of the others—even that creepy old lady. Frankly, he always felt like she looked at him funny.

Hopefully, she didn't have a thing for younger men…

Red glanced at his friend's retreating back, nodded silently to the others, and headed left. He didn't talk much, but that was fine. It didn't matter who the opponent was.

"These youngsters really don't waste time, huh? Guess I'll take the middle route," Giovanni said with a faint smile.

He released a Nidoking from its Poké Ball and then casually stuck his hands in his pockets, heading directly toward the central dormant volcano of Cerise Island.

Compared to Agatha, Lorelei, and Bruno, Giovanni had only one target: Lance.

"You two?"

Dylan turned toward Leaf and Selene as the others disappeared from view.

"Hmm… Want to team up, Selene?" Leaf asked after a moment.

"Sure," Selene agreed easily. The island didn't exactly feel like the safest place.

"Be careful," Dylan said. He wasn't too worried—neither girl was helpless.

"Got it!"

Leaf waved, then headed off to the right with Selene.

Dylan took to the sky on Reshiram, flying toward the volcano's center. It had been a long time since he last saw Lance.

Meanwhile, to the right, Blue made his way along the narrow path. Cerise Island had always been uninhabited, and the overgrown weeds made travel annoying.

Fortunately, his Tyranitar was clearing a path.

Suddenly, Tyranitar halted, growling with a fierce gleam in its eyes.

"Tyranitar!" Blue called, immediately releasing Blastoise to guard him and stepping closer to his partner.

"Ban!"

Tyranitar turned its menacing gaze downward and slammed a foot into the earth.

BOOM!

It wasn't even using Earthquake—just a stomp—but the ground cracked wide open beneath the force.

A moment later, a huge deep-purple cobra slithered up from the ground, cold eyes locked on Tyranitar.

"Arbok… so that means it's Agatha, huh?"

Blue narrowed his eyes. He understood immediately.

"Hehehe… Very well trained—alert and sharp."

A raspy voice echoed through the trees. From behind a trunk emerged a frail old woman in a violet dress, leaning on a cane and smiling eerily.

It was indeed Agatha.

"So full of promise, the youth of today," she said, her face lined but her smile unnerving.

"That old Oak… he must be proud of you."

"You knew my grandfather?" Blue asked, not surprised. Oak was famous throughout Kanto, and Agatha was clearly from his generation.

Come to think of it…

Blue studied her closely, starting to get a bad feeling.

"I've heard Oak doted on you. He was such a strong, admirable man in his prime. But now? He's a pale shadow of who he once was," Agatha said, voice slowly rising in anger.

She truly resented how Oak had quit his trainer career. In their youth, Agatha, Oak, Steel, and Pryce had been close friends. Oak was a powerful trainer and always helped Agatha, despite her abrasive nature.

Over time, she came to admire him—maybe even more than that. But as the years passed, Oak turned his focus to research. Agatha felt abandoned.

Even now, after all these years, she had never forgiven him.

Blue blinked. Something was definitely off about her tone.

Wait—no way… Was this creepy granny one of Grandpa's exes?!

"My grandpa doesn't train anymore. I've never seen him battle," Blue replied, thoughtful. That much was true. For most of his life, Blue had believed Oak was just a researcher. Only later did he learn that Oak had once been the Kanto Champion.

But that was long ago.

"Hehe… That was his choice," Agatha said coldly.

"I tried to change his mind, but the old fool insisted. Disappointing, really."

"Well, that's all in the past now. But today… hehehe…"

Her eerie laughter echoed through the trees.

"Agatha, I'll call you Granny out of respect—since you knew my grandfather."

Blue adjusted his Poké Ball, head tilting slightly, his tone growing cocky.

"I'm not here for a vacation."

"As a current member of the Elite Four, I must ask you to come back to the League with me."

"Oh?" Agatha didn't look surprised at all.

"I know why you're here. Young man, did you really think we wouldn't know about your little raid on Cerise Island?"

"What do you mean?" Blue's eyes narrowed.

"Heh… That Bruno fool thinks he's subtle. Please. He couldn't keep a secret if he tried," Agatha sneered. She'd known about his betrayal for a while now.

"We noticed you the moment you arrived."

"I see," Blue said, understanding now—Bruno was the mole.

"Doesn't matter. We weren't trying to be sneaky anyway."

Blue shrugged.

"But looks like you're not planning to come quietly, Granny. No hard feelings then."

"I don't hold back."

Blue lifted his chin, full of pride.

"My, my… such arrogance. I'm your predecessor, boy—you're just a replacement."

Agatha chuckled darkly.

So he really was Oak's grandson… but compared to Oak in his prime, this kid was nothing.

"Tch. You know what 'former' means? It means 'irrelevant.'"

Blue scoffed.

In his eyes, the former Elite Four were just outdated relics. The current Kanto Elite Four? They were the real deal.

This old hag still thought she could act tough? Laughable.

"Hehehe…"

Agatha laughed softly, but there was no humor in her voice.

Did that brat just call her irrelevant?

Even her patience had limits. How dare this snot-nosed kid speak to her like that?

"Young man… looks like I'll have to teach you what respect for elders really means."

Her eyes narrowed dangerously.

From her shadow, two crimson eyes gleamed—her Gengar rose slowly, grinning wickedly.

"I'm nothing like your grandfather. Let me show you what real battle is."

"Tch… Then let me show you, my dear Agatha Granny—"

Blue flipped his hair and stuck one hand in his pocket, grinning wildly.

"Times have changed!"

Former legends shouldn't be crawling out of retirement. Time for some sweet EXP—and then they could kindly step aside!

(Chapter End)

---
